class="lesson-back" href="/en/course/"> ← All chapters </a> <p class="eyebrow"> 39 · Content revision 10 </p> <h1>Run the scalar reference end to end</h1> <p class="lesson-description">Trace a scalar CPU reference decoder in Rust through validation-selected training, a fixed-fixture comparison over overlapping window-target slots, exact reload, and KV-cached generation. Distinguish that comparison from the unreported policy that would score 442 within-document transitions once each with the longest causal prefix capped at four tokens and only its newest-position distribution; numeric NLL and PPL are not reported for that policy. This integration example does not establish useful language quality or laptop-scale performance.</p> <aside class="lesson-objective"> <strong>In this chapter</strong> <p>Run one deterministic scalar reference decoder end to end on bilingual data, report mean NLL and perplexity for its overlapping test-window slots, distinguish those 1,744 slots from 442 within-document transition occurrences, and preserve the boundaries around selection, regression evidence, checkpoint reload, and cached generation.</p> </aside> <aside class="cheat-sheet" data-cheat-sheet data-cheat-sheet-chapter="39-end-to-end-llm" data-cheat-sheet-page-count="2" data-cheat-sheet-page-statuses="[&quot;Terms 1–10 of 17; page 1 of 2&quot;,&quot;Terms 11–17 of 17; page 2 of 2&quot;]" aria-describedby="cheat-sheet-39-end-to-end-llm-description" data-cheat-sheet-dialog data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <div class="cheat-sheet-panel" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <div id="cheat-sheet-39-end-to-end-llm-pages" class="cheat-sheet-pages" role="region" aria-labelledby="cheat-sheet-39-end-to-end-llm-title" aria-describedby="cheat-sheet-39-end-to-end-llm-page-status" tabindex="0" data-cheat-sheet-pages data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <header class="cheat-sheet-header" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <div data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <p class="cheat-sheet-eyebrow" data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Quick reference</p> <h2 id="cheat-sheet-39-end-to-end-llm-title" data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Run the whole tiny LLM</h2> </div> <button class="cheat-sheet-close" type="button" aria-label="Close cheat sheet" title="Close cheat sheet" data-cheat-sheet-close autofocus data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <span aria-hidden="true" data-astro-cid-ws375rge>&times;</span> </button> </header> <p id="cheat-sheet-39-end-to-end-llm-description" class="cheat-sheet-description" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> A quick reference for the decoder-only LLM pipeline: autoregressive probability, frozen data roles, overlapping window-slot metrics, bitwise training replay, fixed-fixture regression evidence, exact checkpoint restoration, and KV-cached generation. </p> <div class="cheat-sheet-page" data-cheat-sheet-page="1" role="group" aria-label="Terms 1–10 of 17; page 1 of 2" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dl class="cheat-sheet-terms" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Autoregressive factorization</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>The causal decomposition of joint sequence probability into one next-token conditional factor per position, with each factor depending only on the earlier prefix.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Bitwise training replay</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>A repeat of the frozen training run with identical data, initialization, batch order, and arithmetic environment that reproduces every recorded training event and gradient diagnostic, the selected checkpoint, every final model value, and the final optimizer state bit for bit.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Decoder-only LLM</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>An autoregressive language model with a causal decoder stack and no separate encoder, mapping a bounded earlier-token context to next-token logits.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>End-to-end LLM pipeline</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>The course path that turns frozen documents into BPE tokens and causal batches, trains and selects a decoder before one local fixed-fixture evaluation, restores it, and generates text.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Exact checkpoint round trip</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Saving and loading the selected state so checkpoint bytes, model and optimizer bits, BPE ranks, selected step, and RNG state all reproduce exactly.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Exact logit probe</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>A deliberately narrow check that the restored decoder reproduces every logit bit for the fixed At input, without claiming that every possible input was compared.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Fixed-fixture regression evidence</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>A known result rerun to detect changes in checked behavior; later executions retain the lower decoder window-slot mean NLL as a regression condition, so the gap is neither an independent estimate of generalization nor evidence of architecture superiority.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Frozen alpha-one bigram baseline</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>An add-one-smoothed, one-token-context model fitted only on training tokens and frozen before it scores the final ordered test slots.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Frozen document split</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>A fixed assignment of whole documents to training, validation, and test roles, preventing test-reserved text from influencing tokenizer learning, parameter updates, or selection.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Joint sequence probability</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>The probability assigned to an entire token sequence, computed as the product of the observed next-token conditional probability at every position.</dd> </div> </dl> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-page" data-cheat-sheet-page="2" role="group" aria-label="Terms 11–17 of 17; page 2 of 2" hidden data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dl class="cheat-sheet-terms" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>KV-cached generation</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>In this fixture, generation after prompt prefill retains per-block key/value state and decodes only tokens needed for later logits, while matching the complete-prefix reference’s draws, token decisions, stopping, and final RNG state.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Next-token conditional distribution</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>The decoder’s probability distribution over the next token given the allowed earlier prefix; training scores the observed token, while generation samples a new token from it.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Overlapping window-target slot</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>One causal target position identified by document, stride-one window start, and position inside that window. The same within-document transition occurrence can appear in as many as four slots, with the decoder seeing one, two, three, or four in-window context tokens.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Selection-isolated final test evaluation</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>One local post-selection pass whose test targets cannot update parameters or feed a result back into model selection inside that execution; the local access count does not establish repository-wide independence.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Training-only BPE</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>A byte-pair tokenizer whose merge ranks are learned only from training documents, then frozen and applied unchanged to validation and test documents.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Validation-selected state</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>The trained decoder state chosen by validation loss before test mini-batches are materialized.</dd> </div><div class="cheat-sheet-term" data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <dt data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Window-slot mean NLL and perplexity</dt> <dd data-astro-cid-ws375rge>Chapter 39 averages NLL over the same 1,744 overlapping window-target slots for both models, in nats per slot; exponentiating gives dimensionless window-slot perplexity. A separate metric would score each of 442 within-document transition occurrences once, give the decoder the longest available causal prefix capped at four tokens, and use only its newest-position distribution; its numeric mean NLL and perplexity are not reported.</dd> </div> </dl> </div> </div> <nav class="cheat-sheet-pagination" aria-label="Cheat sheet term pages" data-cheat-sheet-pagination data-astro-cid-ws375rge> <p id="cheat-sheet-39-end-to-end-llm-page-status" class="cheat-sheet-page-status" role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true" data-cheat-sheet-page-status <h2 id="predict-the-boundary-before-predicting-the-output">Predict the boundary before predicting the output</h2>
<p>The corpus contains <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">8</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">8</span></span></span></span> training documents, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">2</span></span></span></span> validation documents, and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">2</span></span></span></span>
test documents. The first prediction is about information flow: only the
training documents may teach BPE merge ranks, update decoder parameters, or fit
the count baseline. Validation may choose a trained state. Test evidence may do
neither.</p>
<p>Eight training-only BPE merges produce vocabulary size <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>266</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">266</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">266</span></span></span></span>. With decoder
context capacity <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C=4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em">C</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span> and stride one, the encoded partitions produce <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1820</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1820</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">1820</span></span></span></span>,
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>463</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">463</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">463</span></span></span></span>, and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>436</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">436</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">436</span></span></span></span> overlapping causal windows. Updates use mini-batches of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">16</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">16</span></span></span></span>
windows; the three evaluation epochs
use mini-batches of at most <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>128</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">128</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">128</span></span></span></span> windows, so they contain <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>15</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">15</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">15</span></span></span></span>, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span>, and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span>
mini-batches. The decoder has one block, one head, width <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span>, feed-forward width
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span>, and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1188</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1188</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">1188</span></span></span></span> learned scalar parameters.</p>
<p>Before reading the result, predict:</p>
<ol>
<li>whether two runs from training seed <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>39</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">39</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">39</span></span></span></span> select the same state bit for bit;</li>
<li>whether test loss is visible before that state is fixed;</li>
<li>whether checkpoint reload changes any logit for the frozen <code>At</code>
probe; and</li>
<li>whether cached generation changes a sampling decision.</li>
</ol>
<p>Both training replays select step <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>32</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">32</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">32</span></span></span></span> at validation loss <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>3.889531885</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">3.889531885</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">3.889531885</span></span></span></span>. Only
after selection does the run materialize test mini-batches and give them to one
local final evaluator. Every one of the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>436</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">436</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">436</span></span></span></span> overlapping test windows contributes
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C=4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em">C</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span> target slots:</p>
<span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" display="block"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>436</mn><mo>⋅</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1744.</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{\mathrm{slot}}=W_{\mathrm{test}}C=436\cdot4=1744.</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em">N</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.109em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathrm mtight">slot</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em">W</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathrm mtight">test</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em">C</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">436</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em"></span><span class="mbin">⋅</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">1744.</span></span></span></span></span>
<p>A window-target slot is identified by its document, window start, and position
inside the window. Because adjacent windows overlap, one within-document
transition occurrence can enter the score in as many as four slots. The decoder
sees one, two, three, or four in-window context tokens at the four causal slot
positions; <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C=4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em">C</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span> is its capacity, not the context length of every prediction.
The bigram always uses only the immediately preceding token.</p>
<p>Across the two documents, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span> transition occurrences appear in one slot, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span> in
two slots, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span> in three slots, and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>430</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">430</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">430</span></span></span></span> in four slots. The multiplicities
recover the reported denominator:</p>
<span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" <p>The decoder and frozen alpha-one bigram score the same ordered <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1744</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1744</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">1744</span></span></span></span> slots,
including those repetitions. Their reported values are mean NLL in nats per
slot: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>3.866087547</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">3.866087547</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">3.866087547</span></span></span></span> for the decoder and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>3.981342714</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">3.981342714</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">3.981342714</span></span></span></span> for the bigram. The
window-slot perplexity is the exponential of the corresponding mean:</p>
<span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" display="block"><semantics><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">PPL</mi><mo>⁡</mo></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>exp</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mtext> ⁣</mtext><mrow><mo fence="true">(</mo><msub><mi>ℒ</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo fence="true">)</mo></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{PPL}_{\mathrm{slot}}
=\exp\!\left(\mathcal L_{\mathrm{slot}}\right).</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" class="mord">.</span></span></span></span></span>
<p>The exact window-slot perplexities are <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>47.755180205</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">47.755180205</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">47.755180205</span></span></span></span> for the decoder and
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>53.588940583</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">53.588940583</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">53.588940583</span></span></span></span> for the bigram. Perplexity is dimensionless.</p>
<p>Thus <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>3.866087547</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">3.866087547</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">3.866087547</span></span></span></span> is mean NLL, not perplexity. The mean-NLL gap is
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>0.115255167</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.115255167</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">0.115255167</span></span></span></span> on this fixed fixture. Scoring the same ordered slots makes that
within-fixture comparison fair for this slot-weighted protocol.</p>
<p>The same two test documents contain <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>444</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">444</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">444</span></span></span></span> encoded tokens and therefore <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>442</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">442</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">442</span></span></span></span>
within-document transition occurrences:</p>
<span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" display="block"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><munder><mo>∑</mo><mrow><mi>d</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mi>𝒟</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></munder><mrow><mo fence="true">(</mo><mo stretchy="false">∣</mo><msup><mi>z</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">∣</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn><mo fence="true">)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>444</mn><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>442.</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{\mathrm{transition}}
=\sum_{d\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{test}}}\left(\lvert z^{(d)}\rvert-1\right)
=444-2=442.</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em">N</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3175em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.109em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathrm mtight">transition</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:2.5522em;vertical-align:-1.4022em"></span><span class="mop op-limits"><span class="vlist-t style="top:-2.357em;margin-left:-0.0278em;margin-right:0.0714em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.5em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size3 size1 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathrm mtight">test</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" large-op">∑</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:1.4022em"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em"></span><span class="minner"><span class="mopen delimcenter" style="top:0em"><span class="delimsizing size2">(</span></span><span class="mopen">∣</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.938em"><span style="top:-3.113em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mopen mtight">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">d</span><span class="mclose mtight">)</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">∣</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em"></span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mclose delimcenter" style="top:0em"><span class="delimsizing size2">)</span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em"></span><span class="mord">444</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">442.</span></span></span></span></span>
<p>A conventional once-per-transition metric would score each occurrence once. It
would present the decoder with the maximal available prefix of at most four
immediately preceding tokens and use only the distribution at the newest
position. Chapter 39 does not report that metric’s mean NLL or perplexity, so
the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1744</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1744</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">1744</span></span></span></span>-slot values must not be read as corpus scores over <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>442</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">442</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">442</span></span></span></span> transitions.</p>
<p>The lower decoder slot mean-NLL ordering is retained across later executions, so it is useful
regression evidence. It is not an untouched independent estimate of
generalization or evidence of architecture-wide decoder superiority.</p>
<p>The <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>30994</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">30994</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">30994</span></span></span></span>-byte checkpoint re-encodes to the same bytes and restores the model,
optimizer, tokenizer, and random state exactly. A separate probe
<code>At</code>, encoded as <code>[67,118]</code>, reproduces every logit bit.
Generation then starts from prompt <code>A</code>, encoded as literal token ID
<code>67</code>. Sampling with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>τ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.8</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tau=0.8</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1132em">τ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">0.8</span></span></span></span>, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k=4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0315em">k</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span>, and seed <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>38</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">38</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">38</span></span></span></span> chooses literal
IDs <code>[260,34,34]</code>, decoded as <code>т␠␠</code>, where each
<code>␠</code> marks one generated space. Cached and complete-prefix paths use
the same draws and make the same decisions.</p>

<h2 id="one-product-connects-every-next-token-decision">One product connects every next-token decision</h2>
<p>The complete model still answers the same question introduced near the start of
the course:</p>
<span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" display="block"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>θ</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></msub><mo mtight">1</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3.05em"></span><span><span class="mop op-symbol large-op">∏</span></span></span><span style="margin-right:0.0278em">θ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">∣</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mrel style="height:0.1774em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span></span>
<p>The sequence probability <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>θ</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">P_\theta(z_{1:T})</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em">P</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0278em">θ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">1</span><span class="mrel mtight">:</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em">T</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> is a product of conditional
probabilities. Training minimizes the negative log of those conditionals;
validation selects <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\theta</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em">θ</span></span></span></span>; final evaluation measures the already selected
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\theta</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em">θ</span></span></span></span>; and generation samples one new <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z_t</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5806em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> from the conditional distribution
given <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mrow><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z_{&lt;t}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6079em;vertical-align:-0.1774em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mrel mtight">&lt;</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1774em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>.</p>
<p>The factorization is a causal promise. The probability for position <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6151em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span></span></span></span> may
depend on <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mrow><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z_{&lt;t}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6079em;vertical-align:-0.1774em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mrel mtight">&lt;</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1774em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, never on a later token. Here <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mrow><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z_{&lt;t}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6079em;vertical-align:-0.1774em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mrel mtight">&lt;</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1774em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> names the whole earlier
prefix in the general factorization; this bounded decoder actually receives at
most the retained last <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C=4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em">C</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span> earlier tokens. A frozen split makes the
experimental promise parallel to the mathematical one: test-reserved documents
cannot affect tokenizer learning, updates, or validation selection in this run.
That within-execution boundary does not make a known result independently held
out again when a later execution reuses it.</p>
<p>The test comparison instantiates that factorization with an explicit observation
list. If slot <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6595em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">i</span></span></span></span> contains observed target <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z_i</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5806em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3117em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and the context actually visible
at that slot is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_i</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5806em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3117em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, then the reported mean is</p>
<span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" display="block"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>ℒ</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi>N</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></mfrac><munderover><mo>∑</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></munderover><mi>log</mi><mo>⁡</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>θ</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>∣</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L_{\mathrm{slot}}
=-\frac{1}{N_{\mathrm{slot}}}
\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\mathrm{slot}}}\log P_\theta(z_i\mid c_i).</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathcal">L</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0278em">θ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3117em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">∣</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">c</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3117em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mord">.</span></span></span></span></span>
<p>This sum has <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1744</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{\mathrm{slot}}=1744</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em">N</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.109em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathrm mtight">slot</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">1744</span></span></span></span> equally weighted terms. Equal slot weight
does not mean equal transition weight, because overlap repeats a
within-document transition occurrence. The separate <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>442</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">442</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">442</span></span></span></span>-term policy would
score each occurrence once, use the longest available causal prefix capped at
four tokens and only its newest-position distribution, and report no numeric mean NLL
or PPL here.</p>

<h2 id="keep-sequence-position-separate-from-pipeline-stage">Keep sequence position separate from pipeline stage</h2>
<ul>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>θ</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">P_\theta</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em">P</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0278em">θ</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is the probability distribution defined by the decoder.</li>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\theta</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em">θ</span></span></span></span> contains all learned parameter values chosen by validation loss.</li>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z_{1:T}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5806em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">1</span><span class="mrel mtight">:</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em">T</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is one sequence from its first token through token <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em">T</span></span></span></span>.</li>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em">T</span></span></span></span> is the number of tokens whose joint probability is being assigned.</li>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msubsup><mo>∏</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>T</mi></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\prod_{t=1}^{T}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.2809em;vertical-align:-0.2997em"></span><span class="mop"><span class="mop op-symbol small-op" style="position:relative;top:0em">∏</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.9812em"><span style="top:-2.4003em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span><span class="mrel mtight">=</span><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.2029em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em">T</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2997em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> multiplies one conditional term at every position.</li>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6151em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span></span></span></span> is the current token position.</li>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z_t</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.5806em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is the observed token at that position.</li>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mrow><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">z_{&lt;t}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6079em;vertical-align:-0.1774em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.044em">z</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.044em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mrel mtight">&lt;</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1774em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is the earlier causal prefix in the general factorization; this
fixture presents at most the last <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C=4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em">C</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span> earlier tokens to the decoder.</li>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{\mathrm{slot}}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em">N</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.109em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathrm mtight">slot</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is the number of overlapping window-target slots scored by
each model; here it is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1744</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1744</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">1744</span></span></span></span>.</li>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>ℒ</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal L_{\mathrm{slot}}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathcal">L</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathrm mtight">slot</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is mean NLL in nats per slot, and
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">PPL</mi><mo>⁡</mo></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{PPL}_{\mathrm{slot}}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mop"><span class="mop"><span class="mord mathrm">PPL</span></span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathrm mtight">slot</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is its dimensionless exponential.</li>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{\mathrm{transition}}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em">N</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3175em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.109em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathrm mtight">transition</span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> counts <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>442</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">442</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">442</span></span></span></span> within-document transition occurrences.
The separate policy would score each once with the longest available causal
prefix capped at four tokens and only its newest-position distribution; its numeric
mean NLL and PPL are not reported.</li>
</ul>
<p>The training step number is not <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6151em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span></span></span></span>. A step updates <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\theta</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em">θ</span></span></span></span> from a mini-batch;
<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6151em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span></span></span></span> indexes a token inside the language-model factorization.</p>

<h2 id="from-short-count-contexts-to-autoregressive-transformer-llms">From short count contexts to autoregressive Transformer LLMs</h2>
<p>A count-based bigram estimates the next token from one preceding token and cannot share statistical strength through learned features or use the longer causal prefix.</p>
<p><a href="https://www.jmlr.org/papers/volume3/bengio03a/bengio03a.pdf">A Neural Probabilistic Language Model</a>
supports this bounded claim: Bengio and colleagues describe traditional n-gram generalization through short overlapping sequences and show a neural probability function that learns distributed word representations and benefits from longer contexts; their model is not a Transformer or this course pipeline.</p>
<p><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1506.02629">Generalization in Adaptive Data Analysis and Holdout Reuse</a>
supplies the evidence warning. Dwork and colleagues show that adaptive repeated
reuse of a standard holdout can overfit that holdout; this general warning does
not establish any fact about the capstone fixture, its score, or its local access
count.</p>
<p><a href="https://arxiv.org/pdf/1706.03762">Attention Is All You Need</a> supports a later
boundary: Vaswani and colleagues define the Transformer and mask decoder self-attention so a position cannot read later positions; their published architecture is an encoder-decoder model and does not define this course data, checkpoint, or generation policy.</p>
<p><a href="https://arxiv.org/pdf/2005.14165">Language Models are Few-Shot Learners</a>
supplies the scaled-LLM context: Brown and colleagues train GPT-3, a
175-billion-parameter autoregressive Transformer language model based on the
GPT-2 architecture, and evaluate zero-, one-, and few-shot tasks without gradient
updates or fine-tuning; none of its scale or capability results transfers to
this tiny teaching run.</p>
<p>Neural language models learned distributed token features and longer-context probability functions; the Transformer supplied masked self-attention, and later autoregressive Transformer language models scaled that training objective.</p>
<p>This course capstone combines training-only tokenizer learning, causal
next-token updates, validation-selected state, within-execution selection
isolation, fixed-fixture regression evaluation, exact checkpoint round-trip, and
stateful generation; these are local evidence rules, not requirements of the
cited papers.</p>
<p>Compare the training-only alpha-one bigram with the validation-selected causal
decoder on the same ordered overlapping window-target slots. Four tokens is the
decoder’s context capacity; the four slot positions expose one through four
in-window context tokens. The mean-NLL gap retained by later executions is fixed-fixture
regression evidence, not causal attribution to context or attention and not an
independent generalization estimate. Bengio and colleagues’ discussion of
overlapping sequences does not define this course’s stride-one slot weighting.</p>
<p>Count n-grams provided a strong short-context baseline; learned distributed
features and masked self-attention made longer learned computation possible, and
scaled autoregressive Transformers became one major family of modern LLMs. This
capstone demonstrates local end-to-end responsibility boundaries at inspectable
scale while treating the known lower decoder window-slot mean NLL only as a regression
condition for this fixed fixture.</p>
<p>The executable contrast is narrow: a one-token count context and a decoder with
four-token context capacity score the same <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1744</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1744</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">1744</span></span></span></span> repeated window slots on this
frozen corpus. The lower slot mean NLL verifies the retained fixed-fixture
ordering. The separate policy would score the <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>442</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">442</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">442</span></span></span></span> transition occurrences once
each with the longest causal prefix capped at four tokens and only its newest-position
distribution, but its numeric mean NLL and PPL are not reported. The ordering is
also not an untouched independent estimate of generalization, a universal
architecture ranking, or evidence of useful generation quality.</p>
